--- Commit Message ---
LU-17916 clio: switch to struct folio

Switch cl_page from struct page and page based API to
struct folio and the folio API.

The default folio order of 0, in the absence of fgf_set_order() and
mapping_set_folio_order_range(), on the buffered I/O path the folio
allocation size remains PAGE_SIZE [order 0]

The DIO bounce buffer now allocates folios of order 0 to align
with the existing logic.

User pages used for DIO can be embedded in large folios so
add support for identifying and tracking the page number inside
a large folio from user pages.

Introduce cl_page_batch* and clarify oti_cl_batch as pending
cl_page objects available for cl_commit_cbt to release additional
grant space in osc_queue_async_io(), other users of oti_fbatch
are moved to their respective local stack.

Fix bug introduced in filemap_get_folios_contig() improper handling
of number of folios returned. Also ensure that ref'd folio is released
upon llite.*.dump_page_cache completion for the case where _start() or
_next() is called without a subsequent _show()

Fix bug introduced in conversion to folio_batch addition of pgno to
vvp_set_batch_dirty() which changed the bit-wise flags from i which
walked every bit to pgno which did not advance properly.

HPE-bug-id: LUS-12384
Fixes: ca992899d55f ("LU-16351 llite: Linux 6.1 prandom, folios_contig, vma_iterator")
Fixes: b82eab822c07 ("LU-17081 build: Prefer folio_batch to pagevec")
Signed-off-by: Shaun Tancheff <shaun@tancheff.com>

--- Message (Patrick Farrell, 2025-02-18 17:24:57.000000000) ---
Patch Set 5:

I really think we can do it with checkpatch - it's what we've done before.

We could screw it up, but that's always possible.

--- Message (Timothy Day, 2025-02-18 19:08:20.000000000) ---
Patch Set 5:

> Patch Set 5:
> 
> I really think we can do it with checkpatch - it's what we've done before.
> 
> We could screw it up, but that's always possible.

To be precise, I'm talking about preventing the use of the folio compat APIs [1]. Matt Wilcox called out those APIs in particular.

If we know for certain that some APIs will be replaced/removed, it's seems ideal to discourage usage as soon as possible. Eventually, the kernel itself will remove these folio compat APIs. So if we can get Lustre strictly deprecate them beforehand, it will save us a lot of effort down the line. I think a compile time check will be followed more strictly than a checkpatch warning. People writing new code with old APIs could cause a lot of wasted effort that we could avoid.

I agree that we should start with checkpatch. But I guess I don't see any downside to a stricter check?

[1] https://elixir.bootlin.com/linux/v6.14-rc3/source/mm/folio-compat.c

--- Message (Shaun Tancheff, 2025-02-28 06:15:03.000000000) ---
Patch Set 8:

> Patch Set 8:
> 
> (3 comments)
> 
> What will be our ability to allocate large folios (e.g. 1MB+) for cases when clients are doing large streaming read/write operations?  Will this get us there, or this is just plumbing in the the basic APIs and more work still needed?

Eventually yes. From el9.5 and any 6.11 kernel we just flag the mapping with the maximum order we can handle and on each write_begin() we set the size of the I/O [ideally aligned with the stripe so we do not have to deal with a lot of truncated/split folios].

> 
> No pressure, I'm just trying to understand these changes better?

--- Message (Gerrit AI review for Lustre, 2026-07-28 13:27:28.000000000) ---
Patch Set 42:

(16 comments)

AI code review results below, please doublecheck

Some significant concerns, see inline comments. The folio conversion looks mostly mechanical, but a few hunks change behaviour rather than representation: writing folio->index on pinned user pages in the aligned DIO path, replacing iov_iter_revert() with open-coded arithmetic, and moving the write-commit batch out of osc_thread_info so osc_queue_async_io() no longer sees it. Those three are independent of the folio switch and would be much easier to review (and to bisect) as separate patches. vvp_set_batch_dirty() also no longer compiles on kernels where HAVE_ACCOUNT_PAGE_DIRTIED is set, so the RHEL8-era build is likely broken.

--- Message (Gerrit AI review for Lustre, 2026-08-22 17:04:58.000000000) ---
Patch Set 49:

(6 comments)

AI code review results below, please doublecheck

Mostly good, minor comments inline. The page->folio conversion itself traces correctly: every cacheable path still allocates order-0 folios, so cl_page_from_folio()'s index==folio->index contract and the new LASSERT(folio_nr_pages(folio) == 1) in ll_invalidate_folio() hold on every caller I followed.

The one genuinely new mechanism - sub-page indexing via cp_pgno/cdp_pgno[] - is only reached when a DIO user buffer is backed by a multi-page folio, and nothing under lustre/tests/ does direct I/O from a MADV_HUGEPAGE or hugetlbfs buffer. Would it be worth adding a sanity case that mmaps a huge-page-backed buffer and does an O_DIRECT read/write through it, so folio_page_idx() actually returns something non-zero in regression testing?

--- Message (Patrick Farrell, 2026-09-06 18:29:30.000000000) ---
Patch Set 53:

(1 comment)

Found a real bug here while tracking down a "sleeping in
atomic" warning the Janitor attributes to patches further up
this relation chain.  Details in the inline comment on
vvp_io.c; summary and evidence below.

vvp_set_batch_dirty() zeroes the saved interrupt flags
immediately before restoring them, so it returns with
interrupts disabled on the CPU.  One line:

    flags = 0;
    xa_unlock_irqrestore(&mapping->i_pages, flags);

Everything after that in the write syscall runs with IRQs off.
The first thing that notices is the mutex in
ll_inode_size_lock(), reached from ll_merge_attr() at the end
of ll_file_io_generic(), which is why the warning points there
rather than here.

How I got to it.  On a rocky8.10 VM with 58097 ps53 and nothing
else, instrumented ll_inode_size_lock() to log irqs_disabled()
and dump_stack():

  BUG-equivalent: irqs_disabled=1 in_atomic=0 preempt_count=0 comm=cp
    ll_inode_size_lock+0xa6/0xe0 [lustre]
    ll_merge_attr+0x15/0x40 [lustre]
    ll_file_io_generic+0xf9c/0x11f0 [lustre]
    do_file_write_iter+0x7c8/0xc70 [lustre]
    new_sync_write / vfs_write / ksys_write

in_atomic()=0 with irqs_disabled()=1 is the giveaway: a
spin_unlock_irqrestore() puts preempt_count back to zero but
restores EFLAGS from the value passed in, and with flags==0 the
interrupt-enable bit is clear.

Bisected it with probes: clean before cl_io_loop(), IRQs off
after; clean after __generic_file_write_iter(), off after
vvp_io_write_commit() -- which reaches vvp_set_batch_dirty()
through write_commit_callback().

A plain "cp /etc/hosts /mnt/lustre/f" reproduces it every time.
Deleting the "flags = 0;" line and changing nothing else:
runtests goes from hitting my 10-warning cap to zero
occurrences, PASS 1 (98s).

Scope: the block is inside #ifdef HAVE_ACCOUNT_PAGE_DIRTIED.
rocky8 defines it; rocky9 does not (it takes the
filemap_dirty_folio() branch instead), and a full runtests on
rocky9 with this patch produced no occurrences.  So this is
el8-and-older only.

Impact is understated by the warning being debug-only.  On a
production kernel there is no message at all -- the task simply
continues with interrupts disabled until something re-enables
them, which for a syscall path is the next context switch.

Evidence for it being from this patch rather than further up:
"flags = 0;" is an added line in this change, and counting the
BUG in runtests client syslogs across the chain gives 58097
ps53 = 119, 67791 ps7 = 115, 65094 ps33 = 121, 68541 ps10 =
112, 68619 ps4 = 111, against 0 for an unrelated change on
master running the same debug kernel.  The Janitor only labels
it a NEW warning on the patches at the top of the chain, which
is misleading.

Investigation done with Claude Code.

Patch Set 53: Code-Review-1

Marking this -1 for the vvp_set_batch_dirty() issue I detailed in my previous comment: the "flags = 0;" before xa_unlock_irqrestore() leaves the CPU with interrupts disabled for the rest of the write syscall.

Reproduced on rocky8.10 with ps53 alone (a plain cp is enough), root caused by bisection, and confirmed fixed by deleting that single line -- runtests goes from repeated warnings to none, PASS 1 (98s). It is el8-and-older only, since el9+ compile the filemap_dirty_folio() branch instead.

Happy to clear this as soon as the line goes; it is a one-line change and the rest of the patch is unaffected.

Investigation done with Claude Code.

Patch Set 58:

(10 comments)

AI code review results below, please doublecheck

Mostly a mechanical page->folio conversion and it reads as correct; the reference counting (folio_attach_private() in vvp_page_init() balanced by folio_put()/folio_batch_add() in cl_page_free()) and the new cdp_pgno tracking both trace out cleanly. One hunk is not mechanical (vvp_dev.c) and is worth answering, the rest inline are optional cleanups for whenever this is next refreshed.

The genuinely new behavior here is per-page tracking inside a large folio for DIO user buffers (cdp_pgno / cp_pgno). Nothing in lustre/tests exercises that - an O_DIRECT read/write from a buffer backed by a THP or hugetlbfs mapping would be the case where a non-zero pgno actually occurs. Worth adding a sanity case so the new tracking has coverage.
